## 2026-05-02 14:35 -- [T-P0-689] [MLI-D2] Logistic Regression handwritten numpy in ml_coding (Sigmoid + Stable BCE + GD)
- **What I did**: Created idempotent INSERT-or-UPSERT seed `scripts/seed_logistic_regression_20260502.py` that adds a new `problems` row (title='Logistic Regression (Sigmoid + Stable BCE + GD)', category='ml_coding', difficulty='medium', source='ml-coding-handwritten-2026-05-02', framework_node_id=211) following the K-Means(1064) / KNN(1106) / LR(1102) section structure (题目描述 / 核心代码 / 关键要点 / 面试追问 / 复杂度). Notes body contains a vectorized `LogisticRegression` class with: numerically-stable `_sigmoid` (positive/negative branch split to keep every exp() argument in (-inf, 0]), the EXPLICIT stable BCE per-sample formula `max(z, 0) - z*y + log(1 + exp(-|z|))` IN CODE per task spec (not just the words "log-sum-exp"), full-batch GD with gradient `(1/n) X^T (sigma(Xw) - y)`, L2 regularization with bias-not-penalized via `w_reg[0] = 0.0`, and `predict_proba` / `predict` with tunable threshold for class imbalance. Plus a sibling `SoftmaxRegression` class with stable softmax (subtract row-wise max BEFORE exp), one-hot targets, and the same `(1/n) X^T (P - Y)` gradient form generalized to (d, K). Discussion section (~16KB total notes) covers: (1) the FULL stable-BCE derivation showing why the |z| trick avoids overflow on z<0 AND log(0) on extreme p; (2) sigmoid derivative sigma'(z)=p(1-p), Hessian X^T diag(p(1-p)) X strict PSD => LR is convex => GD globally optimal; (3) class imbalance ladder (threshold tuning > class weights/pos_weight > resampling/SMOTE > focal loss); (4) L1/L2 regularization with subgradient sign(w) for L1 and bias-masking; (5) Newton/IRLS as quadratic-converging Hessian inverse vs GD's linear convergence, L-BFGS as quasi-Newton fallback for large d; (6) Platt scaling, isotonic regression, temperature scaling for calibration; (7) LR vs Naive Bayes (discriminative vs generative), LR vs SVM (logistic vs hinge loss); (8) OvR vs multinomial softmax for K-class. Complexity: O(Tnd) GD, O(nd^2 + d^3) Newton, O(d) prediction. Also added `{ dbId: 1107, title: "Logistic Regression (Sigmoid + Stable BCE + GD)" }` entry to ML_PROBLEMS in `src/frontend/src/pages/QuickIndex.tsx` between KNN(1106) and the end of the list. Note on framework_node link: task spec called for `framework_node_problems` join-table INSERT but that table does NOT exist in this codebase's schema (verified: only `framework_nodes` + `problems.framework_node_id` FK on the row itself); used the FK column directly, which is the canonical link for this DB and is enforced by the seed via the precondition check `framework_nodes.id=211 must exist`.
- **Deliverables**: NEW `scripts/seed_logistic_regression_20260502.py` (~440 lines incl. ~16KB notes body); NEW DB row `problems.id=1107` (description=912 chars, notes=15964 chars, framework_node_id=211); NEW `scripts/_smoke_logistic_regression.py` (extracts the embedded code block and runs functional tests); MODIFIED `src/frontend/src/pages/QuickIndex.tsx` (+1 line in ML_PROBLEMS array, now 4 entries: K-Means/LR/KNN/LogReg).
- **Sanity check result**: (a) **Idempotency 1st run**: `[INSERT] 'Logistic Regression (Sigmoid + Stable BCE + GD)' id=1107 description=912 notes=15964 chars framework_node_id=211`. (b) **Idempotency 2nd run**: `[SKIP] id=1107 ... description+notes byte-equal (desc=912 notes=15964 node=211)` -- 0 writes confirmed. (c) **Embedded-code functional smoke test** (`_smoke_logistic_regression.py`): regex-extracts the python code block from the seed file, exec's into a namespace, runs (i) binary LR on 400 separable Gaussian-blob points -> train_acc=1.0000, final_bce=0.0088 in 500 iters; (ii) stable BCE on extreme logits z=[1000,-1000,0,50,-50] -> finite loss 0.138629 (no nan/inf); (iii) stable sigmoid on the same -> [1, 0, 0.5, 1, 1.93e-22] all in [0,1] no overflow; (iv) softmax LR on 450 3-class blobs -> train_acc=1.0000; (v) stable softmax on Z=[[1000, 999, -1000], [0, 0, 0]] -> [[0.731, 0.269, 0], [0.333, 0.333, 0.333]] rows sum to 1, no overflow. ALL 5 checks pass. (d) **Lint**: `ruff check scripts/seed_logistic_regression_20260502.py scripts/_smoke_logistic_regression.py` -> All checks passed (smoke uses file-level `# ruff: noqa: N806` since it intentionally mirrors ML notation X, Y, P, Z from the seed's markdown). (e) **Backend tests**: `pytest tests/ -x -q` -> 1227 passed in 49s (no regressions). (f) **TypeScript**: `tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` -> exit 0 (frontend type-checks clean). (g) **Framework node link**: precondition check confirms `framework_nodes.id=211` ('Logistic Regression Loss') exists; new problem's `framework_node_id=211` is set on INSERT. (h) **Style invariants**: no emoji, ASCII-only sentinel `<!-- LOGISTIC_REGRESSION_20260502 -->`, Chinese narration + English-term-expansion (binary cross-entropy, log-sum-exp, generalized linear model, Newton's method, IRLS, quasi-Newton, L-BFGS, Platt scaling, isotonic regression, focal loss, MAP, Gaussian/Laplace prior), KaTeX block math `$$L_i = \max(z_i, 0) - z_i y_i + \log(1 + e^{-|z_i|})$$` for the boxed stable-BCE formula and gradient/Hessian, fenced ```python code blocks. ## 2026-05-02 14:50 -- [T-P0-690] [MLI-D3] Geometric Median (Weber problem) handwritten Weiszfeld + Vardi-Zhang
- **What I did**: Created idempotent INSERT-or-UPSERT seed scripts/seed_geometric_median_20260502.py that adds problems.id=1108 (title='Geometric Median (Weber 问题, L2 距离和最小)', category=ml_coding, source=ml-coding-handwritten-2026-05-02) following the K-Means(1064)/KNN(1106)/LR(1102)/LogReg(1107) section structure. Notes (~9.7KB) include: (1) opening 'Inclusion Criterion' rationale per task spec -- ml_coding includes (a) ML algos OR (b) numerical-optimization with ML/stats applications, geometric median qualifies under (b) since Weiszfeld is IRLS / GD on a convex L2-distance-sum and is M-estimator/robust mean used in robust regression and robust clustering init; (2) explicit L1/L2/L2^2 contrast table cross-linking to db://262 Best Meeting Point, with the '1D L2 reduces to ordinary median' degeneracy called out and the 'L2 in 2D does NOT decouple' insight that's the heart of the question; (3) Weiszfeld iteration in numpy with FULL Vardi-Zhang 1999 degeneracy correction (Mathematical Programming 90(3), pp. 559-566) -- when iterate hits sample a_j with multiplicity eta_j, compute T(x) over the regular subset and R(x) = ||subgradient excluding singular term||; if R<=eta_j then Vardi-Zhang Theorem 2.1 certifies a_j IS the geometric median (early return), else use the gamma=max(0, 1-eta/R) convex combination toward T(x); (4) follow-ups covering mini-batch SGD scaling for large N, k=1 K-Means relationship (L2^2->mean, L2->geometric median), 1D=median, breakdown point comparison (centroid=0% vs geometric median=50%, with concrete N=100 numerical example), Weiszfeld=IRLS family, Newton on smoothed objective. Cross-link block appended to problems.id=262 notes under <!-- GEOMETRIC_MEDIAN_CROSSLINK_20260502 --> sentinel pointing back to db://1108. Functional smoke test (_smoke_geometric_median.py) regex-extracts the embedded Weiszfeld code and runs 4 scenarios: symmetric points -> origin (pass), 99 inliers + 1 outlier at (1e6,1e6) -> centroid pulled to ~10000 while geometric median stays near origin |x|<5 (robustness pass), 5 copies of (0,0) + 1 stray at (10,0) -> Vardi-Zhang correctly certifies origin via R=1 <= eta=5 (degeneracy pass), 1D [1,2,3,4,100] -> 3.0 (1D=median pass). All checks pass. ML_PROBLEMS entry added to QuickIndex.tsx (now 5 entries: K-Means/LR/KNN/LogReg/GeometricMedian).
- **Deliverables**: scripts/seed_geometric_median_20260502.py (NEW, ~340 lines), scripts/_smoke_geometric_median.py (NEW, ~85 lines), src/frontend/src/pages/QuickIndex.tsx (+1 ML_PROBLEMS entry), data/mle_prep.db (problems.id=1108 INSERT, problems.id=262 cross-link APPEND).
- **Sanity check result**: 1227 backend pytest pass; ruff clean on both new scripts; tsc clean on frontend; idempotency verified -- second run = 0 writes (both rows report SKIP); functional smoke test passes 4/4 scenarios including Vardi-Zhang degeneracy and 1D-median reduction.
- **Status**: [DONE]
- **Request**: task_db.py update T-P0-690 --status completed
## 2026-05-02 15:30 -- [T-P0-691] [MLI-E1] Rotate Image rectangular n*m generalization (append-style extension)
- **What I did**: Created idempotent APPEND-style seed scripts/seed_rotate_image_rect_extension_20260502.py that adds a rectangular n*m generalization section to problems.id=73 (Rotate Image, LC 48) WITHOUT touching the existing 697-char square content. New 5.5KB section is sentinel-guarded by `<!-- ROTATE_IMAGE_RECT_20260502 -->` and includes: (1) 题目推广 with the in-place semantic distinction (n=m means same n^2 memory; n!=m means same nm linear memory but different row/col lengths, why LC 48 limits to square); (2) three solution layers -- 主答案 = O(nm) auxiliary (open m*n new array, the white-board answer), 特殊情形 = O(1) for any-shape 180deg via involution (i,j)<->(n-1-i, m-1-j) and square 90deg via H circ T, 理论极限 = O(1) for rectangular 90deg via Cate-Twigg 1977 cycle leaders on sigma(k)=kn mod (N-1); (3) D_4 dihedral table of all 8 elements with their composition formulas (R_90 = H circ T, R_180 = H circ V = V circ H, R_270 = V circ T = T circ H) and which 4 elements remain shape-preserving (and thus O(1) in-place) under rectangular n!=m -- {e, H, V, R_180}; (4) explicit tetrahedral-group warning with the motivating context: A_4 (12-order alternating group, tetrahedron rotations) and S_4 (24-order full tetrahedron symmetry) are unrelated to matrix rotation -- a real terminology slip from the original brief, not abstract pedantry; (5) complexity lower-bound table with all four (shape, rotation) combinations and their tight Theta(nm) bounds; (6) honest closing -- 面试可达的最优 -- which says the white-board answer for rectangular 90deg is O(nm) auxiliary, theoretical O(1) is FFTW-grade and should be cited (Cate-Twigg / Brenner / FFTW lineage) but not implemented. Citations verified real during planning: Cate, E. G. & Twigg, D. W. (1977) ACM TOMS Algorithm 513, 3(1):104-110; Brenner (1973) CACM Algorithm 467, 16(11):692-694.
- **Deliverables**: NEW scripts/seed_rotate_image_rect_extension_20260502.py (~265 lines incl. ~5.5KB notes append-block); MODIFIED data/mle_prep.db (problems.id=73 notes 697 -> 6160 chars, append-only).

## 2026-05-03 01:55 -- [T-P1-715] AR-16 cold-start fast-fail watchdog (setsid pgid + SIGTERM grace + race-with-AR12 AC)
- **What I did**: Settled T-P1-715 -- the AR-16 implementation in `scripts/autonomous_run.sh` and the `tests/test_autonomous_wrapper.sh` AC harness were already on disk uncommitted (carried over from the AR-12/15/18 hardening session); this session verified the implementation against all 8 ACs, ran the test suite, and committed. AR-16 wraps each `claude -p` attempt in `_run_claude_attempt()`: launches via `setsid timeout --kill-after=10s` so the inner process becomes a session leader (its own pgid), spawns a background watchdog that sleeps `CLAUDE_P_COLDSTART_GRACE` (default 120s) and then checks whether `logs/autonomous.log` has grown by at least `CLAUDE_P_COLDSTART_GROWTH_MIN` bytes (default 200) -- if not, sends `SIGTERM` to the whole pgid (`kill -TERM -- -$pgid`), waits 4s for log flush, then `SIGKILL`s if still alive, writes one telemetry line to `logs/wrapper-stats.jsonl` with `branch=coldstart_kill`, and touches a marker file. After the main `wait`, the wrapper checks the marker -> sets `_AR16_LAST_KILLED=1`. The outer `run_claude_with_timeout` treats exit codes 137/143 the same as 124, then -- critically -- if `_AR16_LAST_KILLED=1`, refreshes `wrapper_start_porcelain_hash` to the current state BEFORE the AR-12 comparison, so killed-run residue does NOT trigger a false AR-12 extension on retry (AC 5 race-with-AR12). Single-mechanism cleanup: explicit `kill $watchdog_pid` after main wait (no `trap RETURN` per design review). Platform fallback: precheck for `setsid` at script load; if missing (Windows MSYS), auto-set `CLAUDE_P_DISABLE_COLDSTART_GUARD=1` with WARN message -- wrapper falls back to plain `timeout claude` and AR-7's full-timeout retry remains active.
- **Deliverables**: MODIFIED `scripts/autonomous_run.sh` (+115 lines: AR-16 docblock, hyperparams `CLAUDE_P_COLDSTART_GRACE`/`CLAUDE_P_COLDSTART_GROWTH_MIN`, `setsid` precheck, `_run_claude_attempt()` helper with watchdog, race-with-AR12 porcelain refresh, 137/143 exit-code handling on both AR-7 main loop and AR-12 extension path). NEW `tests/test_autonomous_wrapper.sh` (+377 lines: 8 ACs covered -- AC 1 `setsid` precheck/auto-disable, AC 7 kill-switch path, AC 5 race-with-AR12 source-grep + gating, AC 6 telemetry JSON schema, AC 2/3/4/8 end-to-end with mocked `claude` requiring real `setsid` (skipped on Windows MSYS as designed)). Tests run from sandboxed `mktemp -d` with a fake `claude` in PATH; sandbox seeded as a git repo so `git rev-parse HEAD` works inside the wrapper.
- **Sanity check result**: (a) `bash -n scripts/autonomous_run.sh` clean. (b) `bash tests/test_autonomous_wrapper.sh` -> 9 PASS / 0 FAIL / 4 SKIP (the 4 SKIPs are AC 2/3/4/8 end-to-end on Windows MSYS where `setsid` is absent -- the precheck path correctly fires; ACs 1/5/6/7 + sanity for AC 5 logic + telemetry-format AC 6 all run unconditionally). (c) Existing AR-12/AR-18 classify tests `bash logs/_test_wrapper_classify.sh` -> 15/15 PASS (no regression on `_classify_head` 5-outcome state machine). (d) `python -m pytest -q` -> 1232 passed, 0 failed in 42.62s. (e) Source-level invariants verified: race-with-AR12 block exists at the right point in `run_claude_with_timeout` (after `outcome=$(_classify_head ...)`, before AR-12 extension gate), gated on `_AR16_LAST_KILLED`. (f) Telemetry schema matches AR-12 shared spec: `{ts, host, branch, log_growth_b, grace_s, growth_min_b}`. (g) Kill switch `CLAUDE_P_DISABLE_COLDSTART_GUARD=1` confirmed to bypass watchdog entirely (no telemetry written, `_AR16_LAST_KILLED=0`).

## 2026-05-03 20:30 -- [T-P0-721 followup] KNN math delimiters: $$..$$ -> $..$ for inline (fixes TL;DR rendering)
- **What I did**: User reported TL;DR formula rendering broken after the third-pass rewrite. Root cause: `MarkdownPreview.tsx:121` configures `remarkMath` with `singleDollarTextMath:true` -- meaning `$..$` = inline text math, `$$..$$` = display block math (always block-level, even mid-paragraph). My TL;DR was a single flowing prose paragraph with 9 inline `$$..$$` math expressions, each rendered as a block break. Compared the working golden problems: K-Means (1064) uses `$$..$$` ONLY inside `>` blockquote bullets where each math sits on its own `> ` line (display-block doesn't fight bullets); LR (1102) uses `$..$` inline everywhere (matches the existing `feedback_math_formatting` memory). Switched docs/drafts/knn_golden_v1.md from 49 `$$..$$` pairs down to 1 (the genuine display equation `$$\hat{y} = \frac{\sum w_i y_i}{\sum w_i}$$` in Regression section, which sits on its own line) plus 48 `$..$` pairs. Verified via 4-layer test stack: (1) DB byte read; (2) curl backend `GET /api/problems/1106`; (3) offline `unified` pipeline with same plugin chain (`remark-parse` + `remark-gfm` + `remark-math {singleDollarTextMath:true}` + `remark-rehype` + `rehype-raw` + `rehype-katex`) on the live fixture; (4) headless Playwright Chromium opening `http://localhost:5173/quick-index?section=ml`, clicking the KNN card, and DOM-querying the rendered `<p>` containing "Lazy learner". All four layers showed 9 KaTeX spans, 0 katex-errors, 0 raw `$` chars, single contiguous `<p>`. ## 2026-05-04 01:35 -- [T-P0-727] Geometric Median note v2: GD-lens reframe + adaptive-lr/Weiszfeld bridge
- **What I did**: In-place rewrite of `docs/drafts/geometric_median_golden_v1.md` (problems.id=1108) per the GD-lens spec. Title + sentinel preserved; v1 sklearn-style class skeleton dropped in favor of top-level `_weiszfeld_step` + `_vardi_zhang_step` + `fit` functions. New Section 1 ("Derivation") establishes f convex-but-non-smooth and gradient as a sum of N unit vectors (norm <= N globally bounded), then introduces the subdifferential at sample points and the KKT optimality condition R <= eta_j -- this becomes the geometric foundation for both the adaptive-lr bridge and the Vardi-Zhang certificate. New Section 2 ("Plain GD") gives the 4-line numpy GD that "just works" but with constant-lr tuning pain (far-point grad ~ N vs near-optimum unit-vector cancellation -> any single lr is bad). Section 3 derives Weiszfeld as the GD update with eta_t = 1 / sum(1/d_i): the (x - p_i)/d_i terms collapse algebraically to the weighted-barycenter fixed point -- "Weiszfeld is parameter-free adaptive-lr GD". Section 4 keeps the existing _vardi_zhang_step code from v1 but reframes its derivation through the Section 1 KKT story (R = ||sum_{i not in J} u_i|| <= eta_j is the optimality certificate; otherwise gamma * T + (1-gamma) x is projected subgradient). Section 5 fit drops the class skeleton -> top-level `fit(points, max_iter=200, tol=1e-7) -> ndarray`. Section 6 is a 3-column comparison table (Plain GD / Adaptive-lr Weiszfeld / Vardi-Zhang). Cheat sheet: cut "no closed form" Q (subsumed by sec 1 first-order condition); added "Constant-lr GD why-hard, fix-is-what" Q; kept mean/coord-median, convergence, breakdown, large-N, k=1 K-Means bridge (5 Qs total). End-to-end test updated to call `median = fit(points)` (function call, not class .fit).
- **Deliverables**: MODIFIED `docs/drafts/geometric_median_golden_v1.md` (full rewrite within v1 sentinel, char count 6751 body / 6796 payload incl. sentinel); MODIFIED `data/mle_prep.db` `problems.id=1108.notes` (6686 -> 6796 chars via existing seed UPDATE path).

## 2026-05-04 22:00 -- [T-P0-737] Escape pipe in doc=90 table row 7 (Find Words O-complexity)

- **What I did**: Discord ad-hoc bug report from xushenghui: doc=90 (Meta AI-Native Coding Inventory hub, company_id=31) row 7 "Find Words Containing Other Words" rendering broken on `/companies/31/prep?tab=docs&doc=90`. Root cause: math expression `$O(\sum |w_i|)$` in column 4 contained two raw `|` characters that the GFM table parser treated as column delimiters, splitting that row into 8 cells instead of 6 -- the `[打开 -> Find Words](db://1104)` link got pushed out of the "完整题解" column entirely. Fix: replace `|w_i|` with LaTeX `\lvert w_i \rvert` (visually identical absolute-value/cardinality bars but no pipe collision) in seed `scripts/content_meta_anc_inventory_hub.py:91`. Per Invariant 3 the seed is the source of truth; never patched DB directly.

(c) Pipe-count check on the 8-row table: header + separator + 8 data rows all uniformly have 7 `|` chars = 6 cells; row 7 specifically now ends with the `db://1104` link in column 6 as intended. ## 2026-05-06 21:36 -- [T-P1-784] Add LC 777 Swap Adjacent in LR String 题解 + Google + index
- **What I did**: User Discord msg 1501803587943010336 dropped a tight Python solution for LC 777 inline (no attachment), flagging it as 智力题. Solution distills to two structural invariants: (1) filter X out, L/R sequences must be identical char-by-char (L/R can't cross each other since every swap involves an X); (2) L-start-idx >= L-target-idx (L only moves left via `LX -> XL`), R-start-idx <= R-target-idx (R only moves right via `XR -> RX`). LC 777 row did NOT exist in problems (not in bulk import). ## 2026-05-07 -- [T-adhoc-lc215-hoare] LC 215 quickselect Hoare double-end pointer + 3-way partition variants
- **What I did**: Discord ad-hoc request: existing LC 215 notes only had Lomuto single-pointer partition (every `< pivot` element triggers a swap). Added a Hoare double-end-pointer variant where `i`/`j` scan from both ends with inner `while nums[i] < pivot: i++` / `while nums[j] > pivot: j--` to skip already-positioned elements; only swaps misplaced pairs (avg swap count halved). Also added a 3-way (Dutch-flag) follow-up section for duplicate-heavy inputs where the equal-to-pivot region is recursion-skipped. Wrote idempotent UPSERT seed `_update_lc215_hoare_quickselect_20260507.py` keyed on leetcode_id=215 (notes_len 1872 -> 5127); kept existing Lomuto + Min Heap solutions intact, slotted new sections as 解法一-优化 and 解法一-加强.
- **Deliverables**: scripts/_update_lc215_hoare_quickselect_20260507.py (new). DB row `problems.id=99` notes column updated. MLI commit `900ee1d` (1 file, +230 lines).
- **Sanity check result**: (a) Both new implementations validated against `sorted(arr)[-k]` ground truth on 9 hand-written cases (LC samples + edge cases: single-element, all-duplicates, reverse-sorted, sorted, binary 0/1 array) -- zero failures. (b) Random stress test 500 cases (n=1..30, values -50..50, k=1..n) -- zero failures. (c) Re-running seed yields deterministic notes_len=5127 (idempotent UPSERT). (d) Documented two common Hoare-partition pitfalls inline (pivot must NOT be `nums[lo]`/`nums[hi]`; outer-loop boundary `hi = p` not `p - 1`).
- **Status**: [DONE]
- **Request**: No task_db change (ad-hoc, no T-* assignment).
